金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 19 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 美國 9 月通膨升溫，Fed 年底前再升息機率仍高： 美國 9 月 CPI 年增 3.7%，高於 8 月的
3.4%；核心 CPI 年增率亦由 2.4%升至 2.6%，顯示通膨壓力不只來自能源，核心價格亦有回
升。隔日的 PPI 年增率則由 5.6%升至 6.3%，反映上游成本壓力仍強。通膨重新升溫，使Fed
即使面對就業降溫，也難以快速轉向寬鬆，市場對年底前再度升息的預期仍高。
◼ 台積電財報創新高，AI 需求強勁但市場轉向檢視融資風險：台積電 10/15 公布其第三季淨利

達 7,631 億元、年增 69%，連續第六季刷新紀錄，第三季美元營收 467.2 億美元，並預估第
四季營收將升至 523～543 億美元 ； 公司同時將2026 全年營收成長預估提高至約 44%， 反映
AI 晶片與先進製程需求仍極為強勁。然而，高利率環境使市場開始檢視 AI 資料中心業者的槓
桿與融資能力，CoreWeave 約 500 億美元債務引發信用疑慮，引發市場波動。
◼ 中東衝突再度推高能源風險 ， 油價高檔加劇全球通膨壓力 ：胡塞武裝上週攻擊沙烏地阿美設施 ，
伊朗亦表示採取新的海上攻擊策略，使荷莫茲海峽與波斯灣航運風險再度升高。10/16 布蘭特
原油與西德洲原油期貨雙雙收在近三週高點。
市場展望
◼ 科技股中期趨勢料將不變 ， 但短線波動勢將加劇 ：近期市場同時面臨通膨升溫 、AI 資本支出疑
慮與重要科技財報公佈在即等不確定因素，短線風險偏好可能受到壓抑。在利率維持高檔及市
場評價偏高的環境下，市場短線料將隨財報結果與市場預期而出現較大波動。然而，上週台積
電與 ASML 財報結果整體優於預期 ， 可見AI 相關企業營運與資本支出尚未出現全面轉弱跡象 ，

科技產業基本面仍具一定支撐，惟後續宜更加重視企業獲利、現金流及資本支出變動及科技子
產業的表現分化現象。
◼ 中天期美債與投資級債債券兼顧息收與利率風險：美國經濟仍具韌性 ， 加上政府與企業發債供
給維持高檔，可能使市場利率持續處於較高水準。雖然投資級債信用利差位於相對低檔，但企
業獲利、現金流及償債能力仍具支撐，加上較高的美國公債基準利率，使投資級債的利息收益
仍具吸引力。相較之下，短天期債券面臨未來利率下降後的再投資風險，長天期債券則較容易
受到公債供給增加 、 期限溢酬及利率波動影響 ， 因此中天期美國公債與投資債券較能兼顧息收
與利率風險。

Investment Thesis & PT methodology and risks

Investment Thesis - Unimicron Technology 
Unimicron is a key supplier of ABF substrates (25% market share in 2025) and BT 
substrates and also produces FPC/HDI/PCB products. We note (1) over 70% of ABF 
shipments are covered by long-term agreements (LTA), which limits pricing flexibility and 
makes price hikes less likely; (2) the company’s execution track record has been weaker 
than peers; (3) capacity expansion plan remains slower than peers, constraining its 
ability to capture incremental AI demand. The current ABF substrates market is seeing 
more favorable supply demand outlook, and Unimicron is likely to see a better pricing 
outlook to reflect rising material costs in the coming quarters. AI ABF accounts for 10% 
of the company’s total revenue, but the growth is likely to be slower than peers as the 
company is losing market share given its less favourable production yield and slow 
capacity expansion. Overall, we are Neutral rated on Unimicron given our outlook for 
limited upside potential, barring ASIC AI servers, despite our generally positive view in 
the long term on its profitability trajectory. 
Price Target Risks and Methodology - Unimicron Technology 
Valuation methodology: Our 12-month target price of NT$1,270 for Unimicron is 
based on 17.4x 2028E P/E, which is 2.7x STDV higher than the past upcycle average P/E 
(10.3x).  
Key upside/downside risks: (1) Slower/faster-than-expected PC demand recovery, (2) 
Slower/faster-than-expected ABF substrates upgrading pace, and (3) Slower/faster 
than-expected AI server PCB market share loss progress.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

Company Report 
公司研究報告 
本報告僅供宏遠投顧內部及客戶參考，雖已力求正確與完整，但因時間及市場客觀因素改變所造成產業、市場
或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保
證。非經本公司同意，不得將本報告加以引用或轉載

1 
6239 力成(Powertech)

報告日期： 2026/10/28 研 究 員：翁浩軒  (Davy Wong)  
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 先進封裝比重提升，FOPLP 預期 2027 年放量貢獻 
 記憶體供需仍緊缺，稼動率維持高檔 
 訂單能見度高，預期明年營收獲利創高

一. 3Q26 毛利率優於預期，EPS 3.44 元 
力成 3Q26 營收 253.34 億(QoQ+9.6% 、YoY+26.87%) ，受惠於 DRAM
及 Logic 需求強勁，營收均季增約 15%，Nand 營收亦季增 5%。各
產品而言，DRAM 方面，訂單能見度高，推升營收穩定成長; Nand
方面，AI 伺服器帶旺 eSSD 需求，推升營收季增年增; Logic 業務
方面，出貨續增，營收季增年增雙位數; 超豐受到客戶需求強勁，
產品價格調整及簽訂 L T A 帶動營收季增年增雙位數;  T e r a  
Probe/TeraPower 營收持續季增年增雙位數。3Q26 毛利率受惠稼動
率提升而上升至 24.02%優於預期，唯營業費用較原先預期高，稅
後淨利 26.11 億(QoQ+17.71%、YoY+69.87%)，EPS 3.44 元。

二、4Q26 營收小幅季增，預估 EPS 4.18 元 
4Q26 預期記憶體、邏輯業務維持穩定成長，營收預估季增中低個
位數。DRAM 方面，記憶體產業仍處於供不應求加上客戶晶圓產
出極大化有利封測整體利用率，原材料成本上漲將適度反映至客
戶售價; NAND 方面，AI 帶動 SSD 及 Nand 封測需求維持高檔，且
隨封裝材料成本上升，力成將適度反映於封測價格上; Logic 業務
方面，受惠高階封裝 FCBGA 需求持續成長，整體產能利用率維持
高檔，FOPLP 客戶端產品驗證中，預期 2027 年量產。子公司 Tera 
Probe/Tera Power 受惠 HPC、AI、ADAS 及車用產品應用持續需求
的支撐，營收穩健成長，子公司超豐於 AI 與 HPC 帶動 Bumping
及 FC 封裝需求，菲律賓廠第四季起貢獻營收。預估力成 4Q26 營
收 262.21 億(QoQ+3.5%、YoY+22.49%)，毛利率 24.3%，稅後淨利
31.72 億(QoQ+21.46%、YoY+70.24%)，EPS 4.18 元。

三.  2026 / 2027 年獲利創高，預估 EPS 12.97 元/17.79 元 
展望 2026 年，AI 相關應用帶動公司 Memory 及 Logic 業務需求強
勁，預估今年逐季成長。Memory 業務方面，DRAM 及 Nand 封測
受惠於客戶拉貨積極，加上車用、網通、Server 等需求成長下，記
憶體位元出貨量穩定成長;隨著 AI 功能逐漸滲透到邊緣端及 AI 
投資評等：買進

PC 、AI 手機，預期將有助於推升 Memory 需求。Logic 業務方面，
公司在 Flip Chip 、Bumping 等高階封裝產能增加，且在客戶滲透率
慢慢提高，搭配子公司超豐、Tera Probe/Tera Power 需求升溫，營
收持續成長。預估力成 2026 年營收 959.85 億(YoY+28.1%)，毛利
率 22.54%，稅後淨利 98.47 億(YoY+77.881%)，EPS 12.97 元。展望
2027 年 ， 記憶體供不應求延續 ， 加上 AI 帶動 logic 晶片封裝需求，
營收獲利持續創高，公司積極投入高階封裝業務，基於其記憶體
封裝薄晶片與晶片堆疊之技術開發新型態封裝產品，公司投入之
FOPLP 可整合高階 IC 功能提高效能，降低成本，2027 年將明顯貢
獻營收。預估力成 2027 年營收 1166.97 億(YoY+21.58%)，毛利率
23.57%，稅後淨利 135.05 億(YoY+37.14%)，EPS 17.79 元。

四.投資建議：買進，目標價 320 元 
投資建議方面，力成近年逐步從傳統記憶體封測跨入邏輯業務，
並拓展高階封裝，成為全方位封測廠。公司受惠於記憶體封測及
高階封裝需求，今明年營運正向，考量到 1)公司在先進封裝與國
際大廠合作，FOPLP、光通訊將成為新成長動能、2)明年記憶體供
需仍緊缺，稼動率維持高檔、3)今明年營收獲利創高，參考 2027
年 EPS 給予 18 倍本益比，目標價 320 元，建議買進。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
力成科技設立於 1997 年 5 月，專注於記憶體之封裝測試業務，為
全球第五大封測廠。公司聚焦記憶體產品，在大股東全球最大記
憶體模組製造廠商金士頓的支持下，與國際大廠策略結盟，如美
光、東芝、Intel、Sandisk 等，而取得量大且穩定訂單。公司記憶
體封測同業包括日月光、矽品、南茂、華東、福懋等公司。公司
2011 入股超豐，邏輯 IC 封測佔營收提高到 20％以上(主攻通訊市
場之高階邏輯封測)。2025 年營收比重分別為：封裝占比 67%，測
試占比 22%、SIP/Module 占 11%。從產品類別看，動態隨機存取記
憶體(DRAM)占比 22%，快閃記憶體(Flash)占比 25%，邏輯 IC 占比
42%、SIP/Module 占比 11%。

二.產業趨勢概況  
1. 力成持續擴充先進封裝佈局，FOPLP 將於 2027 放量 
力成為全球第一大記憶體封測大廠 ， 客戶為全球 DRAM 、 Nand Flash
大廠，近年公司積極拓展 logic 業務，營收比重已占四成以上，預
估未來邏輯業務將穩定貢獻營收，公司積極推進高階封裝佈局，
提供 2.5D、3D IC 封裝產品，其中 FOPLP 已取得客戶認證，公司
將以 FOPLP 提供 AMD AI 晶片，隨著機台陸續到位，預估 2027 年
上半年開始量產，因應客戶需求及訂單能見度提升，新購入 P12
廠區(購自友達)無塵室已建置完畢，將做 on substrate 的部分 ，而因
應 FOPLP 產品測試需求，子公司 TeraPower 亦新購置廠房，用於

CP 、FT 測試，預估 2026~2027 年將建 400~500 台測試機台。另外，
公司亦與 Broadcom 在新加坡成立合資公司 ， 以力成在 FOPLP 封裝
經驗基礎下，共同開發及生產先進封裝基板上之加成式細線寬之
重佈層封裝技術，另外 Broadcom 亦帶領力成進入光通訊領域，預
期今年底小量生產 microbump Based OE 、 2027 下半年開始生產 CPO 
Swtich、2028 年量產 CPO AI 晶片。未來力成將跳脫傳統記憶體封
測廠，提供全方位封測服務。

2. AI 帶動 Logic 及 Memory 業務需求強勁 
AI 及雲端基礎建設投資強勁，持續支撐半導體及記憶體產業需
求，目前 DRAM 與 NAND 需求持續大於供給，產品價格趨勢有利
於記憶體產業營收成長 ， AI 相關應用將持續帶動公司成長 。 DRAM
方面，AI 強勁需求使整體記憶體仍然供不應求，整體產能利用率
可望維持高檔水準，並待客戶新晶圓產能陸續開出。隨著市場供
需持續緊俏，客戶對原物料成本上漲帶動的價格調整亦展現高接
受度。 Nand &SSD 方面， AI 應用推動企業級 SSD 及 NAND 相關需
求維持強勁，而封裝材料成本持續上升，力成將適度反映於客戶
價格，預期對營收及毛利率具正面效益。

Logic 業務方面， AI 市場持續增溫，力成在高階 FCBGA 同步受惠，
既有高階產品需求亦持續擴大，而先進封裝 FOPLP 方面，客戶端
產品驗證持續推進，預期 2027 年量產，此外隨著市場需求及量產
信心度提升，客戶在新產品導入 (NPI) 的需求亦顯著增加，力成除
持續推進 5 倍 reticle 的封裝應用外 ， 更進一步投入超大尺寸封裝技
術的研發，以因應未來 AI 晶片對高效能、高整合度的大尺寸封裝
需求。在高階 FCBGA 領域，第三季成功導入大晶片 (1x reticle Size)
封裝 (75x78) 的量產後，第四季將進一步跨入更大尺寸封裝領域
(78x88) ，整合 SoC Die 與 DRAM 元件在單一基板上。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

1、3Q26 毛利率優於預期，EPS 3.44 元 
力成 3Q26 營收 253.34 億(QoQ+9.6% 、YoY+26.87%) ，受惠於 DRAM
及 Logic 需求強勁，營收均季增 15%，Nand 營收亦季增 5%。毛利
率受惠稼動率提升而上升至 24.02%優於預期，唯營業費用較原先
預期高，稅後淨利 26.11 億(QoQ+17.71%、YoY+69.87%)，EPS 3.44
元。

2、4Q26 營收小幅季增，預估 EPS 4.18 元 
4Q26 預期記憶體、邏輯業務維持穩定成長，營收預估季增中低個
位數。預估力成 4Q26 營收 262.21 億(QoQ+3.5%、YoY+22.49%)，

研 究  報 告                
力成(6239.TT) 
產業：電子–半導體 
4Q26 營收維持高檔，關注高階封裝業務布局進度  
 投資評價與建議 
我們認為高階封裝業務進度如期，且為明年公司重點布局業務，整體狀況與原
先預期差異不大；我們給予公司18倍PE，為近期股價修正前之平均評價水準。 
 重點評論分析 
估4Q26營收維持高檔：當前公司封裝、測試產能利用率均達90%以上，推估
營收動能包含：(1)記憶體需求維持高檔，新業務TSV DDR5開始量產，另公司
持續因應記憶體報價上漲 ， 適時反映於封測報價 ； (2)邏輯業務中 ， FCBGA需求
維持穩健且公司將於本季導入更大尺寸封裝 ， 子公司超豐Flip Chip月產能於年
底擴充至2.3億顆，至於Tera Probe/Terapower除了有AI/MML需求支撐外，
⾞用需求回溫亦帶來成⻑動能。估4Q26營收季增5.8%至268億元，毛利率
24.1%，稅後EPS 3.8元。 
高階封裝業務逐步發酵、封測報價議價能力強勁支撐2027年成⻑動能 ：展望
2027年，基於：(1)DRAM市場供不應求至少延續至 2027年，機構估2027年
DRAM報價續漲但漲幅收斂，推估將持續支撐漲價成本之能力；(2)邏輯業務
中，封測產業產能利用率維持高檔，持續支撐公司議價能力，而公司高階封裝
比重成⻑帶動產品組合持續轉佳，如FOPLP明年有望開出3k月產能，子公司
Tera Probe/Terapower持續搶攻AI測試業務，另Terapower新竹新廠將於明
年貢獻營收。預估2027年營收年增22.5%至1,182.4億元，稅後EPS 18元。 
 投資風險 
FOPLP進展不如預期。

| 營運概況  
3Q26本業表現大致符合預期：力成3Q26營收季增9.6%至253.3億元，毛利率24%，稅後EPS 3.44元，其中本業表
現大致符合預期，營收動能來自於記憶體需求維持穩健，子公司超豐價格調漲和客⼾需求成⻑，T e r a  
Probe/Terapower持續受惠AI/MML、HPC封測訂單支撐，而獲利方面則有價格調漲、高產能利用率(力成3Q26封
裝、測試產能利用率分別為85~90%、85%)之挹注，帶動毛利率季增2.3ppts。 
估4Q26營收維持高檔：當前公司封裝、測試產能利用率均達90%以上，推估4Q26營收動能包含：(1)記憶體業務需
求維持高檔，其中企業級SSD持續受惠於AI伺服器需求，另TSV DDR5開始量產，估月營收貢獻約1~2億元，價格
方面，公司持續因應記憶體報價上漲並適時反映於封測報價；(2)邏輯業務中，FOPLP驗證進度如期推進，FCBGA
需求維持穩健且公司將於本季導入更大尺寸封裝(78mm*88mm)，子公司超豐Flip Chip月產能於年底擴充至2.3億
顆 ， 另將部分不具成本優勢之產品轉移至菲律賓廠量產 ， 至於Tera Probe/Terapower除了有AI/MML需求支撐外 ，
⾞用需求回溫亦帶來成⻑動能。我們估4Q26營收季增5.8%至268億元，毛利率24.1%，稅後EPS 3.8元。 
高階封裝業務逐步發酵、封測報價議價能力強勁支撐2027年成⻑動能：展望2027年，基於：(1)DRAM市場供不應
求至少延續至2027年，機構估2027年DRAM報價續漲但漲幅收斂，推估將持續支撐漲價成本之能力，至於NAND
市況據機構預估明年仍有個位數的缺口或趨於平衡，僅有企業級SSD需求仍維持強勁；(2)邏輯業務則為明年最大成
⻑動能，基於整體產業產能利用率維持高檔，支撐公司議價能力，加上高階封裝比重成⻑帶動產品組合持續轉佳，
包含FOPLP明年有望開出3k月產能，推估應用為AMD之電競晶片，子公司Tera Probe/Terapower除了既有業務持
續成⻑外，也積極搶攻美系客⼾下⼀代AI ASIC測試商機，另Terapower新竹新廠也將於明年開始貢獻營收(估月營
收貢獻約2~3億元)。預估2027年營收年增22.5%至1,182.4億元，毛利率24.5%，稅後EPS 18元。

泰合(6467)

承銷價:105.6 
興櫃價(低/高)： 
32.25/302

Target Price:260

泰合主力產品 BEQIA 專為吞嚥困難族群設計 ， 以Apixaban 為成分
的抗血栓口溶膜，鎖定高齡、吞嚥困難及需長期抗血栓治療族群 。
BEQIA 於 2026 年 6 月 22 日重新送件美國 FDA，進入 10 個月
PDUFA 審查期，2027 年 4 月 22 日為審核完成目標日期，可望於
2Q27 取得美國暫時性核准(Tentative Approval)。 歐洲EMA 方面 ，
則於 2026 年 7 月 17 日完成 Day 121 關鍵清單回覆 ， 朝CHMP 最
終審查意見(Day 210)與 EC 核准推進，公司預計 4Q26 可望獲歐盟
上市許可 。授權策略方面，泰合採取原廠與非原廠雙軌並行 。 對原廠
而言，BEQIA 可借力其成熟通路快速推升口溶模市占，並在面臨專
利懸崖與學名藥競爭時，可藉此維持利潤表現，洽談時即以解決原
廠現有困境的定位切入 。 至於非原廠 ， 泰合採精準區域分擊 ， 鎖定主
要市場以外的區域優先展開談判，累積市場實績與聲量。 泰合目前
已在歐美、紐澳、拉丁美洲等地區與數十家藥廠簽訂NDA，並持續
洽談，多家跨國藥廠與各區域BD 代表已進入實質洽談階段。此外，
下一代產品 TAH3341 長效口溶膜雖仍處臨床前，但也有廠商表達
興趣並關注發展進度 。泰合對外競價拍賣 5100 張 ， 競拍底價88 元，
最高投標張數 669 張，每股承銷價 105.6 元。評價上，參考同業有
6576 逸達以及 6838 台新藥，逸達生技近年股價區間 58-141 元，
股本 15.75 億元，市值區間 91.3-222 億元。台新藥近年股價區間
22-70 元，股本 15.1 億元，市值區間 33-106 億元。而泰合目前股
本 6 億元，參考同業市值區間，給予目標價 260 元，給予買進之投
資建議。

➢ 公司簡介： 
泰合生技成立於 2010 年 7 月，為新劑型新藥及特殊學名藥開發廠，藉由自
行開發之藥物傳輸平台技術結合現有藥物成份，將現有口服藥品成分改造為
透皮與透黏膜藥物傳導劑型，以經皮吸收貼片與口溶膜藥品為代表 。公司專
注於開發 505(b)(2)NDA 新劑型新藥，並藉由 Transepithelial Delivery 
System (TDS)藥物傳輸技術平台改良現有藥物缺點，主要包括透皮及透黏膜
兩種不同藥物傳輸平台，劑型主要包含經皮吸收貼片、口溶膜與口頰溶膜三
類。產品線涵蓋抗血栓藥品、鴉片過量解毒及戒毒劑、小兒過動症、化療止
吐等治療領域。截至 2025 年，營收來自已授權之學名藥及受委託開發之專
案收入 99%。

抗凝血藥物市場: 
在全球醫療環境中，心血管疾病為主要致死與致殘原因之一，其發生率隨人
口老齡化及現代生活型態改變而持續上升。相關疾病涵蓋冠心病、高血壓及
高血脂等，並可能進一步導致心肌梗塞與中風等重大臨床事件。根據世界衛
生組織（WHO）統計，約有五分之四之心血管疾病死亡與心臟病發作及中
風相關，顯示其對全球公共健康之重大影響。

在臨床治療上，抗凝血藥物為心血管疾病管理中不可或缺之核心用藥之一，
主要用於預防及控制血栓形成，以降低心肌梗塞、中風及其他嚴重併發症之
風險。隨著民眾對心血管健康意識提升，以及疾病預防需求增加，抗凝血藥
物之臨床應用持續擴大，成為全球醫療體系中重要之基礎治療。 
在市場規模方面，全球抗凝血藥物市場於 2025 年約為 414.1 億美元，預計將
持續成長至 2034 年約 970.9 億美元 ， 年複合成長率約為9.93%， 顯示該市場

泰合產品介紹: 
➢ BEQIA(TAH3311 Apixaban ODF)抗血栓口溶膜: 
TAH3311(BEQIA)是全球首創的抗血栓口溶膜新劑型新藥，以 BMS/Pfizer 開
發的 Eliquis 抗血栓錠劑為對照藥物。Eliquis 有效成分為 Apixaban，透過直
接抑制 Xa 凝血因子來治療和預防靜脈血栓 ，根據 BMS 財報 ，2025 年 Eliquis
全球銷售額達 144 億美元，其中美國及美國以外之銷售額分別達 102 億美元
和 42 億美元。Eliquis 佔 BMS 總營收約 29.8%，Eliquis 原料藥專利於 2028
年 10 月 17 日到期，劑型專利於 2031 年 8 月 24 日到期，為 505(b)(2)新劑
型新藥創造良好的切入時點，專利懸崖帶來營收的巨大挑戰，原廠已和解的幾
家學名藥廠預計最快將於 2028 年 4 月上市。Eliquis 除增加小兒用的粉劑外 ，
未開發出新劑型，而研發新一代的 FXIa 抗血栓藥數據臨床三期試驗療效及安
全性數據仍待觀察 ，並且抗凝血錠劑在具有吞嚥困難患者 使用痛點仍無法解
決。

表一：2021 年後原廠 BMS/Pfizer 與 Apixaban 學名藥廠 P4 訴訟和解狀況

資料來源：泰合、第一金投顧整理

TAH3311(BEQIA)為泰合重點開發產品，其主要成分為 Apixaban，並採用口
溶膜（orodispersible film, ODF）劑型設計。Apixaban 透過選擇性抑制凝
血因子 Xa，可有效降低中風與全身性血栓風險，而降低非瓣膜性心房顫動
(NVAF)成年患者發生中風和全身性栓塞的風險是最大的應用，佔 Eliquis 總
銷售額的 70%。BEQIA 口溶膜鎖定改善高齡患者吞嚥困難的用藥痛點，相較
於 Eliquis 是一天吃兩錠需要搭配喝水，BEQIA 是一天吃兩片，僅需放入口
中即可快速溶解完成用藥，不須喝水、沒有噎嗆風險，優化長照與醫療機構
給藥流程。2025 年美國有 1,055 萬名心房顫動(AF)患者，其中約 75%之
791 萬名患者為 65 歲以上高齡患者，這當中 50~75%有吞嚥困難，故
BEQIA 在美國目標病患族群達 395.5 萬~593 萬人，隱含目標市場規模
110~164 億美元。

PDUFA 審查期 ，2027 年 4 月 22 日為審核完成目標日期 ， 可望於2Q27 取得

17 日完成 Day 121 關鍵清單回覆，朝 CHMP 最終審查意見(Day 210) 與 EC
核准推進，公司預計4Q26 可望獲歐盟上市許可。授權策略方面，泰合採取原
廠與非原廠雙軌並行。對原廠而言，BEQIA 可借力其成熟通路快速推升口溶
模市占，並在面臨專利懸崖與學名藥競爭時，可藉此維持利潤表現，洽談時即
以解決原廠現有困境的定位切入。至於非原廠，泰合採精準區域分擊，鎖定主
要市場以外的區域優先展開談判 ， 累積市場實績與聲量 。 泰合目前已在歐美 、
紐澳、拉丁美洲等地區與數十家藥廠簽訂 NDA，並持續洽談，多家跨國藥廠
與各區域 BD 代表已進入實質洽談階段。

資料來源：泰合、第一金投顧整理

➢ TAH3341 長效劑型: 
在抗凝血治療市場中 ， 患者多為高齡且需長期甚至終生用藥族群 ， 臨床上普遍
存在多重用藥及吞嚥困難等問題，進而影響用藥便利性與治療依從性。儘管
Apixaban（Eliquis®）已為目前凝血因子 Xa 抑制劑中之領導產品， 
具備良好療效與安全性 ， 其每日兩次給藥之頻率及傳統錠劑劑型 ， 對部分患者
而言仍存在使用負擔 ， 顯示在既有標準治療基礎上 ， 透過劑型與給藥方式創新
以提升依從性，仍具明確之未被滿足醫療需求。 
TAH3341 為泰合延伸既有 Apixaban 產品線所開發之長效抗血栓口溶膜產
品，結合口溶膜（orodispersible film, ODF ）與先進緩釋技術，將原需每日
兩次服用之錠劑優化為 「每日一次」 之創新劑型。產品可於口腔中迅速崩解並
釋放藥物，使患者無需吞嚥或飲水即可完成給藥；同時，透過精密設計之控釋
機制 ， 使有效成分於體內持續穩定釋放 ， 維持適當血中濃度並達成長時間抗凝
效果。 此一 「快速崩解× 延長釋放」 之雙重設計，有效兼顧給藥便利性與療效
穩定性，並有助於降低血中濃度波動所帶來之潛在風險。 
在產品定位上，TAH3341 於維持 Apixaban 既有療效與安全性基礎上，進一
步透過降低給藥頻次與改善劑型使用方式 ， 顯著提升患者用藥依從性與長期治
療持續性。特別針對高齡族群、吞嚥困難患者及多重用藥族群，此一劑型可有
效降低給藥障礙並優化治療體驗。同時，產品亦有助於簡化用藥流程，降低照
護者負擔並提升整體用藥管理效率。TAH3341 透過 「標準治療藥物× 長效化
設計× 創新劑型平台」之整合策略，不僅回應現有抗凝血治療在依從性與便
利性方面之未被滿足需求 ， 亦進一步強化產品差異化與臨床價值 ， 有望於持續
成長之抗血栓市場中建立具競爭力之產品定位。

### Page 1

Q3 亚⻢逊营收同⽐增 12% 、略低于分析师预期， EPS 同⽐不增反降 3% ，经营利润率逼近
14% 创历史新⾼； AWS 收⼊增速近五年最⾼、预计明后年均增超 40% ； Q3 线上销售仅增
2% 、增速三年半最低，⼴告收⼊较预期低 6% ；资本⽀出翻⼀倍多⾄ 773 亿美元，较预期⾼
逾 30% ；⾃由现⾦流负 424 亿美元、为 Q2 的 5.6 倍； Q4 营收和利润指引中值低于预期。公
司维持今年资本⽀出指引 2200 亿美元不变。股价盘后⼀度跌超 10% 。
摘要：
亚⻢逊三季度财报呈现明显的两⾯性：核⼼经营利润强于预期、备受关注的云计算业务 AWS 保
持⾼速增⻓，但核⼼业务电商增⻓乏⼒，公司总营收和 EPS 盈利均逊于华尔街预期，且 AI 相关资
本开⽀继续维持⾼位，令市场对⾃由现⾦流和投资回报周期的担忧升温。
美东时间 29 ⽇周四美股盘后，亚⻢逊公布，截⾄ 2026 年 9 ⽉ 30 ⽇的今年第三季度，公司净销售
额同⽐增⻓ 12% ⾄ 2019.4 亿美元，略低于分析师预期的约 2022.6 亿美元；稀释后每股收益

（ EPS ）同⽐下降约 3.1% ⾄ 1.89 美元，分析师预期同⽐微增 0.5% ⾄ 1.96 美元；⽽经营利润同⽐
增 61.5% ⾄ 280.8 亿美元，较市场预期⾼逾 7% ，经营利润率升⾄ 13.9% ，为公司史上最⾼⽔平。
财报最⼤亮点仍是 AWS 。三季度 AWS 净销售额同⽐增⻓ 39.3% ⾄ 459.8 亿美元，较分析师预期⾼
1.3% ，创将近五年来最⾼单季增速，季末年化收⼊运转率达 1840 亿美元，环⽐⼆季度末时增⻓
8.9% 。 CEO Andy Jassy 在财报中强调， AWS 在功能和基础设施上的投⼊正在奏效，并预计
2027 年和 2028 年 AWS 收⼊增速都将超过 40% 。

### Page 2

相⽐ AWS ，三季度线上销售仅同⽐增⻓ 2% ，创 2023 年初以来最低单季增速。亚⻢逊对假⽇季、
第四季度销售的指引中值逊于分析师预期也显⽰，其线上业务短期内恐难迅速回暖。
虽然 AWS 收⼊继续劲增，但市场更在意 “ 代价 ” 。亚⻢逊三季度购置物业和设备的资本⽀出⾼达
772.6 亿美元、约为去年同期的 1.2 倍，较市场预期⾼逾 34% ；⾃由现⾦流连续第⼆季录得负值，
从去年同期的流⼊ 147.9 亿美元转为流出 423.6 亿美元，流出规模远超⼆季度。公司还维持约
2200 亿美元的 2026 年全年现⾦资本⽀出指引不变，并未释放 AI 投⼊放缓的信号。
财报公布后，周四本已收跌超 4% 的亚⻢逊股价盘后进⼀步跳⽔，盘后跌幅⼀度超过 10% 。
营收略低于预期，但经营利润率创历史新⾼
从整体收⼊看，亚⻢逊三季度净销售额 2019.4 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 12% ；若剔除汇率影响，增速
为 13% 。
亚⻢逊特别提⽰，今年会员⽇ Prime Day 在公司的多数重要市场地区均提前⾄⼆季度，⽽ 2025 年

Prime Day 完全落在三季度，因此今年三季度同⽐增速受到时点错位影响；若剔除这⼀因素，三
季度收⼊同⽐增速将⾼出约 400 个基点。
利润端则明显更强。亚⻢逊三季度经营利润为 280.8 亿美元，较去年同期的 174.2 亿美元⼤增
61% ，经营利润率从去年同期的 9.7% 提升⾄ 13.9% 。这意味着，即便收⼊略低于预期，公司在
履约、销售营销、管理费⽤等⽅⾯的效率改善，仍然⽀撑了创纪录的经营利润率。

### Page 3

净利润为 206.9 亿美元，低于去年同期的 211.9 亿美元；稀释后 EPS 为 1.89 美元，同⽐下降 3% 。
这 ⼀ 同 ⽐ 下 滑 很 ⼤ 程 度 上 与 去 年 同 期 的 ⾮ 经 营 性 ⾼ 基数 有 关 ——2025 年 三 季 度 亚 ⻢ 逊 因
Anthropic 投资确认了 95 亿美元税前收益，⽽今年同期没有类似收益，其他收⼊ / 费⽤项⽬从去年
同期的正 101.9 亿美元转为负 7 亿美元。
换⾔之，这份财报不是经营层⾯的利润失速，⽽是收⼊和每股收益略低于预期、叠加⾮经营性
项⽬和资本开⽀压⼒，削弱了投资者对短期回报的信⼼。
AWS 继续加速： AI 需求⽀撑云业务，但市场也在追问投⼊回报
AWS 是三季度最亮眼的业务线。该部⻔净销售额 459.8 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 39% ，⾼于分析师预期
的约 454 亿美元，同⽐增速较⼆季度的约 37% 进⼀步加快，继续创 2021 年四季度以来最⾼增速；
AWS 经营利润 177.7 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 55% ，经营利润率为 38.7% ，⾼于去年同期的 34.6% 。

从收⼊结构看，三季度线上销售额 685 亿美元，贡献亚⻢逊三分之⼀的净销售额， AWS 销售额占
总额的 23% ，⾼于去年同期的 18% ，但其贡献了亚⻢逊整体经营利润的约 63% 。这说明亚⻢逊
的利润池仍⾼度依赖云业务，⽽ AWS 的重新加速，对⽀撑集团估值⾄关重要。
财报披露， AWS 近期与 Meta 、英伟达、 OpenAI 、 Salesforce 、甲⻣⽂、⾼通、 Verizon 等签署
了新协议。 Amazon Bedrock 继续快速增⻓，财富 500 强企业在 Bedrock 上的⽀出同⽐接近 7 倍，
整体 token 量同⽐增⻓近 17 倍； Bedrock AgentCore 已有数万名客⼾使⽤，三季度部署的智能体
数量超过前三个季度总和的两倍。
Jassy 对 AWS 前景的表态也⾮常激进。他称 AWS 三季度年化收⼊运⾏率已达 1840 亿美元，并预
计 2027 年和 2028 年 AWS 收⼊增速均超过 40% 。他还表⽰，历史上从未出现过万亿美元收⼊规模

### Page 4

的企业，⽽亚⻢逊未来 5 到 10 年可能拥有两个这样的业务。
但这也是市场分歧所在： AWS 的增⻓说明 AI 投⼊正在转化为需求，但⾼增⻓同时需要巨额算
⼒、数据中⼼和芯⽚投资。投资者并⾮否认 AWS 复苏，⽽是在重新评估这种增⻓需要消耗多少
现⾦流。
AI 资本开⽀成为股价压⼒核⼼：⾃由现⾦流⼤幅转负
资本开⽀是这份财报引发市场负⾯反应的关键。
三季度，亚⻢逊购置物业和设备⽀出为 772.6 亿美元，远⾼于去年同期的 351 亿美元，远超市场
预期的 574.7 亿美元。过去 12 个⽉，公司购置物业和设备净额达到 2106.6 亿美元，同⽐增⻓
82% 。亚⻢逊明确表⽰，这⼀增⻓主要反映了⼈⼯智能相关投资。
现⾦流表显⽰，过去 12 个⽉亚⻢逊经营现⾦流为 1683 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 29% ；但⾃由现⾦流为
流出 423.6 亿美元、流出规模约为⼆季度 76 亿美元的 5.6 倍，⽽去年同期为流⼊ 147.9 亿美元。也
就是说，公司主营业务造⾎能⼒仍在增强，但 AI 基础设施投⼊的速度更快，已经吞噬并反转了⾃

由现⾦流。
资产负债表也体现出扩张压⼒。截⾄三季度末，亚⻢逊现⾦及现⾦等价物为 557.8 亿美元，低于
去年底的 868.1 亿美元；⻓期债务升⾄ 1731.3 亿美元，⽽去年底为 656.5 亿美元。三季度公司新
增⻓期债务融资 481 亿美元，前三季度新增⻓期债务融资 1151 亿美元。
对成⻓型科技公司⽽⾔，资本开⽀本⾝并⾮负⾯，尤其是在 AWS 增速重新上⾏的背景下。但当
三季度资本开⽀⼤幅超预期、⾃由现⾦流显著转负，同时公司⼜维持全年约 2200 亿美元现⾦资
本开⽀预期时，市场⾃然会担⼼： AI 投⼊何时进⼊回报期？未来⼏年是否还会持续挤压现⾦流？
北美和国际业务：收⼊受 Prime Day 错位影响，利润改善明显
零售相关业务表⾯上增速不强，但利润端改善较为明显。
北美业务三季度净销售额 1128.5 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 6% ，略低于市场预期的约 1133.9 亿美元；经
营利润 87.2 亿美元，较去年同期的 47.9 亿美元⼤增 82% ，经营利润率从 4.5% 提升⾄ 7.7% 。

国际业务净销售额 431.1 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 5% ，也略低于预期；若剔除汇率影响，增速为 8% 。
该部⻔经营利润 15.8 亿美元，⾼于去年同期的 12 亿美元，经营利润率从 2.9% 提升⾄ 3.7% 。

### Page 5

亚⻢逊解释称， Prime Day 时点变化压低了三季度收⼊增速。在线商店收⼊ 684.7 亿美元，同⽐
仅增⻓ 2% ；第三⽅卖家服务收⼊ 464.9 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 9% ；实体店收⼊ 57.1 亿美元，同⽐增
⻓ 2% 。全球付费商品数量同⽐增⻓ 12% ，全球运输成本同⽐增⻓ 11% ，显⽰订单量仍在增⻓，
但收⼊结构受到促销时点影响。
因此，零售业务不能简单理解为需求恶化。更准确地说，三季度零售收⼊表现不如市场预期，
但利润率改善和配送效率提升仍然延续。公司称， Prime 会员三季度收货速度创历史同期最快，
超过 15 亿件商品实现当⽇或次⽇送达。
⼴告业务增⻓放缓，是财报中另⼀短板
⼴告业务三季度表现低于预期，是除资本开⽀外另⼀个压制情绪的因素。
亚⻢逊⼴告服务收⼊同⽐增⻓ 11% ⾄ 196.4 亿美元，较分析师预期收⼊ 208.7 亿美元低约 6% ；剔
除汇率影响增⻓ 12% 。这⼀增速相⽐此前多个季度 20% 以上的增⻓明显放缓，也低于市场预期
的约 208.7 亿美元。

公司表⽰，⼴告收⼊受到 Prime Day 时点以及其他若⼲时间因素影响，并预计四季度⼴告收⼊同
⽐增速将回升⾄⾼⼗位数区间。如果这⼀指引兑现，三季度⼴告降速可能更多是时点扰动，⽽
⾮⼴告业务结构性放缓。
不过，市场短期难免会更敏感。⼴告是亚⻢逊近年来利润率更⾼、增⻓更快的业务之⼀，⼀旦
增速放缓，就会削弱投资者对零售⽣态商业化能⼒的信⼼。相⽐之下，订阅服务表现较稳，三
季度收⼊ 140.1 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 11% ，⾼于市场预期的约 136.9 亿美元。
四季度指引不算差，但营收和利润中值低于预期
亚⻢逊给出的四季度指引总体处于华尔街预期附近，但并未带来⾜够惊喜。
公司预计四季度净销售额在 2400 亿⾄ 2470 亿美元之间，同⽐增⻓ 12% ⾄ 16% ，其中包含约 60 个
基点的汇率不利影响。该营收区间的中值为 2435 亿美元，略低于分析师预期的 2445.2 亿美元，
但预期值落在指引区间内。
更关键的是经营利润指引。亚⻢逊预计四季度经营利润为 285 亿⾄ 335 亿美元，⽽市场预期约

331 亿美元。换⾔之，公司指引上限仅略⾼于共识预期，中点 310 亿美元则低于预期。
这解释了为何股价反应如此剧烈：三季度营收和 EPS 均⼩幅不及预期，⼴告收⼊低于预期，四
季度利润指引中点偏弱，同时资本开⽀⼤幅超预期且全年 AI 投⼊计划维持⾼位。 AWS 的强劲增

### Page 6

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

### Page 1

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.38 / 19 October 2026ECONEWS
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
IMF ANNUAL MEETINGS
Resilience, but no relief. The IMF's latest global growth forecast is un-
changed from a year ago and from April 2026 (at 3.1% in 2026 and 3.4%
in 2027), despite the energy shock, however with a different geogra -
phical breakdown: many energy importers and war-exposed countries
downgraded, and AI-exposed countries upgraded. Resilience was the

buzzword of the meetings, however it appeared mostly in the rear -
view mirror. Looking ahead, buffers are running thin, fiscal policy needs
urgent consolidation, and downside risks (from energy and AI exube -
rance) loom large. Global headline inflation is expected to rise to 4.7%
in 2026, mainly due to energy and food, before easing back to 4.1% in
2027 (revised +0.2pp). Growth in advanced economies is revised up to
2.1% in 2026 (+0.4pp, driven by Germany), staying at 1.8% in 2027. Eu-

rozone growth is upgraded to 1.1% in 2026 (+0.2pp) and 1.2% in 2027.
US growth stays projected at 2.3% in 2026 and 2027 (+0.1pp). Germany
sees the largest G7 upgrade, to 1.3% in 2026 (+0.6pp) and 1.1% in 2027
(+0.1pp). France is the only major advanced economy downgraded, to
0.4% in 2026 (-0.2pp) and 0.7% in 2027 (-0.2pp). For more detailed
takeaways see Substack and this week’s EcoWeek editorial.
ADVANCED ECONOMIES
EUROPEAN UNION
Trade policy, competitiveness and budget agreements by year-end.

EU leaders asked the Commission to propose by year-end an instru -
ment allowing, in extreme cases, full closure of the single market to
a rival deemed to be attacking the EU economy. It is designed as a
“second-strike” deterrent against unfair practices and retaliation.
The Commission is also exploring extending the hybrid-vehicle safe -
guards approach agreed with China last week to other sectors such
as chemicals and machinery. On the "Made in Europe" label, vehicles

would need to be assembled in Europe with at least 70% of compo -
nent value (excluding batteries) sourced locally and with substantial
R&D in the EU. The UK would be included, provided London aligns with
EU trade defence measures. EU Council also called for decisive pro -
gress on the competitiveness agenda, with the single-market (inclu -
ding 26th regime) and simplification files to be wrapped up by year-end.
On the multi-annual financial framework, the objective remains a deal

by end-2026, so that legislation can be adopted in 2027.
EURO AREA
Moderate tightening does it. Multiple ECB Governing Council members,
speaking from the IMF Meetings in Bangkok, re-underwrote their
“moderate response” approach to the ongoing energy-driven infla -
tion surge. Accordingly, they signaled that additional rate action was
likely but not imminent, acknowledging that the rise in bond yields
was doing part of the job, and that there were no signs yet of second

round effects. Strengthening the case for moderation, the Eurozone in-
dustrial production fell in August for the second month running, by
0.5% m/m, pulled down by pharmaceutical output and motor vehicles.
That said, production in Netherlands surged to a new high (2.5% m/m
and 8.7% y/y), driven by AI machinery and equipment.
France
Business starts booming, insolvencies are stable, growth outlook is
downgraded. Business creations rose by 16% y/y in the 3 rd quarter

(+23% y/y in September), driven mainly by the digital sector and retail.
In parallel, business bankruptcies stabilised in the 3rd quarter according
to Altares, with a 0.5% increase y/y (compared with a 5.4% increase y/y
in Q2), and their impact on employment declined: 50,510 jobs at risk
in Q3, down 3% y/y (see our recent analysis). The Banque de France
halved its estimate for Q3 GDP to 0.1% q/q (same as Insee) due to
the manufacturing sector. Its monthly survey showed a sharp jump in

business uncertainty in September, driven by fuel prices and economic
deterioration (the most acute readings in industry and services since
April). The OAT-bund spread was rangebound (137-142).
Germany
Producer prices are rising. Wholesale prices rose 8.7% y/y (vs. 6.8% in
August) due to higher wholesale prices for energy products and inter -
mediate goods, particularly metals.
UNITED KINGDOM
Summer outperformance and kudos from IMF. Monthly GDP defied

expectations in August and posted a 0.1% m/m against a -0.1% consen-
sus. This marked a slowdown from July (0.4% m/m), but also a fourth
consecutive month of expansion. Industrial production underpinned
the advance: it accelerated to 0.8% m/m (consensus: -0.1%, prior: 0.3%)
thanks to pharmaceuticals. The IMF sees growth accelerating to 1.3%
in 2026 (+0.3 pp since July) and 1.2% in 2027. It also singled out the UK
among advanced economies for its efforts and progress in putting its

fiscal house in order.
UNITED STATES
Inflation heats up, manufacturing accelerates, but an October hike
is unlikely. CPI inflation exceeded expectations at 0.65% m/m in Sep -
tember (up 0.2pp), the fastest monthly pace since April. Accordingly,
the y/y rate climbed to 3.7% (up 0.3pp). The biggest upside surprise,
however, came from the core CPI: at 0.33% m/m (consensus: 0.2%, prior:
0.3%) and 2.6% y/y (consensus: 2.5%, prior: 2.4%); as services remained

sticky while goods accelerated to 0.3% m/m on autos and AI-related
items. Producer prices too rose well above expectations to 1.3% m/m
(consensus: 0.6%, prior: 0.5%) – the largest monthly increase since
March 2022 – and 6.3% y/y (consensus: 5.3%, prior: 5.6%). Industrial
production accelerated in September to 0.7% m/m (up 0.6pp) thanks
to utilities. Manufacturing returned to growth (0.3% m/m, up 0.5pp)
with gains broadening beyond the AI-related and aerospace sectors.

Retail sales excl. autos and gasoline, by contrast, slowed sharply to
0.2% m/m (down 0.9pp). During his speech at the IMF Meetings after
the hotter than expected inflation prints, Chair Warsh declined to hint
at an imminent rate hike. We continue to expect the next rate hike to
occur in December.
To watch this week:
- Developed markets Flash PMIs for October (Friday).
- Eurozone Germany September PPI, October ZEW (Tuesday), France Insee Business Climate (Thursday).

- UK August Employment data (Tuesday), September CPI and PPI (Wednesday), September Retail Sales (Friday).
- Japan September CPI, October PMI (Friday).

### Page 2

6
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.38 / 19 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
JAPAN
Producer price inflation eases. Producer prices fell for a second month
in a row in September, down a further 0.5% m/m, while the yearly rate
slowed for the first time since February (-0.8pp), though it remains
firm at 6.8%. The IMF forecasts growth at 0.7% in both 2026 and 2027,
broadly unchanged. The yen weakened past 158 per dollar again after

reports that the BoJ is leaning against a back-to-back October rate hike.
EMERGING ECONOMIES
Emerging markets
Asia’s outlook upgraded, other regions downgraded.
The IMF upgraded its Asia growth forecast to 4.8% in 2026 and 4.4% in
2027. The largest upgrades are for economies well integrated into the
AI supply chain, notably Taiwan (+5.5pp to 10.7%), South Korea (+0.7pp
to 3.3%), Singapore, Vietnam, Malaysia and to some extent Thailand.
India is forecast to grow 7% in both 2026 and 2027 (fiscal year basis),

up 0.6 and 0.3pp from July on robust domestic demand.
Central Europe’s GDP growth is projected at 2.1% in 2026, slightly
lower than April’s forecast. In 2027, it remains unchanged at 2.3%.
Growth paths are diverse within the region.
In the Middle East & North Africa, GDP is expected to contract by 2%
in 2026. GCC real GDP should fall by 4.8% with only Oman expected
to post positive growth (4.1%). A marked rebound is expected for 2027
(MENA: 7%; GCC: 9.1%).

The growth outlook for Latin America remains subdued, revised down
to 2.3% in 2026 (from 2.4%) and 2.5% in 2027 (from 2.7%). However, in
its "International Trade Outlook 2026" published on 15 October, the
Economic Commission for Latin America and the Caribbean (ECLAC) ex-
pects the region to experience a trade boom in 2026: goods exports are
projected to surge by 20% in value terms (11% from price increases and
9% from volume growth), with trade to Asia (excluding China) soaring

by 32%, while intraregional trade growth lags behind at 8%. Yet, these
export gains are concentrated in commodities and low-value-added
manufacturing, limiting their spillover to broader growth. Stronger
trade is thus providing only a modest boost to the economy.
China
Explosive export growth, subdued inflation and credit growth. In Sep-
tember, export value growth surged to 36.3% y/y from 25% in August,
driven by semiconductors, computer equipment and other AI goods, as

well as green technologies. Export prices surged, especially for semi -
conductors, while total export volumes remained robust. Import value
growth also accelerated to 31% y/y from 28.2% in August, boosted by
both rising volume and rapidly increasing prices. The trade surplus
hit a new monthly record of USD 134 bn in September. CPI inflation
edged up to 0.9% y/y in September, mainly because of a 14.9% rise in
vehicle fuel prices. Food prices continued to fall (-1.7% y/y) and core

inflation was stable at 1%. Producer price inflation accelerated to 5%
(the highest since 2022) due to rising commodity prices. Growth in to-
tal domestic credit outstanding (“social financing”) continued to slow
down, reaching a record low of 7.1% y/y in September. Bank loans to
households declined (-2% y/y) for the seventh consecutive month.
In Q3 2026, real GDP growth rebounded to 4.5% y/y (1.2% q/q) from
4.3% y/y (0.9% q/q), primarily led by industrial production (which rose by

5.1% y/y on average in Q3 vs. 4.6% in Q2). On the demand side, exports
were the key driver while fixed-asset investment continued to contract.
Retail sales posted a small pickup in September but remained flat
in real terms in Q3 as a whole. With growth reaching 4.6% y/y year-to-
date, China needs only a 4.2% growth in Q4 to meet the official target of
4.5%-5% for this year – which seems easily feasible. The IMF trimmed
its growth forecast by 0.1pp to 4.5% in 2026 and 4% in 2027.

Indonesia
MSCI launched a consultation on reclassifying the country from Emer-
ging Market to Standalone Market status, with a final decision due by
end-June 2027, citing opaque shareholding structures and suspected
coordinating trading. Since January 2026, MSCI had frozen Index ad -
justments and threatened only a downgrade to Frontier market status
(like Pakistan), but market participants argued that a Frontier reclas -
sification would not properly reflect the extent of the market’s pro -

blems. Over the medium term, the risk is heavy foreign capital outflows
at a time when net FDI no longer covers the current account deficit.
This leaves Indonesia reliant on foreign portfolio inflows and the ru -
piah exposed to a reversal in sentiment. On the day of this announce -
ment, the Jakarta Composite Index fell by 11.6%, its steepest drop since
January 1998. Among Asian emerging markets, Indonesia has posted
the worst stock market performance since the beginning of the year

(-40.7%).
COMMODITIES
Iran/Oil. More of the same. Brent oil prices have remained broadly
stable this week (0.5% at 107$/b on Monday). Renewed Iranian at -
tacks on tankers in the Strait and intensification of Houthis’ attack over
the Saudi territory feed the geopolitical risk premium on barrel prices.
But IEA announcement of an accelerated 100 mb oil release (above
the March initial target of 400 mb) and reports that the US may lift
sanctions on the Russian and Iranian shadow fleets have contributed to

crude price stability. The spread with physical barrel (Dated Brent) has
declined (6.7$/b on Oct 16th). Trump administration’s various attempts
to lower diesel prices have had a limited impact for now. European gas
prices (TTF) recorded another weekly rise (4.7% w/w to 88.4 €/MWh,
the highest level since Dec 22nd, 2022) on the back of the rising number
of attacks on vessels in the Gulf and persisting disruption in the Strait.
However, EU gas inventories reached 74% of full capacities on October

16th (i.e., almost at the 80% month-end target), which should reduce
support for TTF prices going forward.
y/y = year-on-year
m/m = month-on-month
bp = basis point
pp = percentage point
mb = million barrels

Document 336: Eurozone_ Improving business sentiment, driven by tech, is spreading to other sectors (2026-10-14)

EUROZONE: IMPROVING BUSINESS SENTIMENT, DRIVEN BY TECH, IS SPREADING TO OTHER SECTORS
Stéphane Colliac & Tarik Rharrab
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
The September PMI surveys confirm that growth in the Eurozone is getting stronger, boosted in particular by new export orders, which are
still rising. Sectors that were already performing well – technology and capital goods (including for defence) – are continuing to grow, while

those that had been struggling until now – aeronautics, consumer goods and financial services – are seeing their production indices recover.
This strong performance is particularly noteworthy, given that it has not yet been undermined by higher inflation.
The heatmap shows the PMI index readings: a reading above 50 indicates expansion, while a reading below 50 signals contraction compared to the previous
month. For each indicator, sectors are ranked in descending order based on their September 2026 reading.

Shades of red represent values above 50, while shades of blue represent values below 50: the intensity of the colour increases the further away they are
from this threshold.
Production growth spreads to more sectors
The PMI surveys on business sentiment confirm that business activity in the Eurozone is expanding at a faster pace and spreading to more sectors.
Household-consumption-related sectors (goods and services), which were adversely affected in the spring by the outbreak of the conflict in the Middle

East, do not appear to be negatively impacted by the rising inflation observed since August. The aeronautics sector, where production has mainly been
limited by supply constraints, is also showing signs of improvement.

These trends, although still moderate, come alongside a drive to upgrade equipment, fuelled by investment in digital technology, electrification
(which is supporting the automotive sector) and defence. The momentum in technology goods and services further strengthened in September.
The production index for computer and electronics hit a five-year high, while the software services production index stayed close to its July peak.

Financial services are also enjoying a significant improvement, reflecting the healthy lending momentum despite interest rates starting to rise
(see our analysis).
Growth driven by exports
Exports drove growth in the first half of 2026, both across the Eurozone as a whole (see our analysis) and among its key members (notably
Germany). This momentum is also increasing, as indicated by the ‘new export orders’ component of the various PMI indices. As was the case with

production, there has been a particularly noteworthy increase for capital goods (technology and machinery) and related services, particularly digital
services. This trend is playing out more broadly, as, in September, new export orders rose across all sub-sectors, with the exception of intermediate
goods and consumer goods, where contraction recently eased. There has been a significant improvement in the transport sector, particularly in
the aeronautics industry, which has major markets in Asia. Recent data on French exports show that aeronautics exports to this region are rising

once again.
The convergence between rising production and increasing export-order backlogs shows that Eurozone growth is based on an increasingly solid
foundation. Inflationary pressures are not holding it back for the time being.

Document 337: Expected 2027 TMT supply points to a new record

European TMT gross bond supply may reach €150bn in 2027,
driven by tech funding for AI and data centres rather than
traditional telecoms. Strong cash ﬂows and credit quality
support issuance, but disciplined funding will be key to market
absorption
Jan Frederik Slijkerman
AI and data centre investment is expected to be the main driver of TMT bond
issuance in 2027
We predict another year of strong supply for the European TMT
sector. We expect €150bn of gross issuance and €103bn of net

issuance, given €47bn of debt redemptions. The additional
issuance is not coming from the traditional telecom or media
issuers, but from global tech companies.
As we expect the data centre build-out to continue in 2027, issuers
are likely to return to the European investment-grade credit market
to raise part of the required funding. Meanwhile, we do not see a
major reason for traditional sectors to reduce or increase

Telecoms supply
We will break down the main factors aﬀecting debt issuance,
starting with FY27 capex requirements. While we expect capex as a
percentage of sales to decline, aggregate capex is likely to increase

slightly. Companies such as Orange and Vodafone are integrating
recent acquisitions and may need to invest additional capital to
realise merger synergies. We therefore do not expect the lower
capex-to-sales ratio to translate into less issuance. Nevertheless,
BT and KPN are exceptions, as both clearly intend to reduce capex
in FY27.
Low-single-digit EBITDA growth gives companies some capacity to
issue additional debt while maintaining stable leverage. However,

higher interest rates provide an incentive to keep debt levels in
check. We therefore expect the increase in debt issuance to be
driven mainly by M&A. Several debt-funded acquisitions of smaller
networks could generate around €5bn of net issuance. A further
€3bn could come from US operators raising euro-denominated
funding for spectrum payments in 2027.

### Page 3

Technology supply
In 2027, we believe the AI build-out will continue. Tech companies
currently report healthy returns on investment, demand for AI
services continues to outpace supply, and companies want to
maintain their market positions. We therefore expect further capex
growth in 2027, with the large hyperscalers investing close to
US$1tr in ﬁscal 2027.
As in 2026, we expect these companies to ﬁnance their
investments largely from operating cash ﬂow, but also through

debt issuance, with additional funding coming from European
markets. We expect hyperscaler data centre operators to issue at
least €60bn in 2027, somewhat above the approximately €35bn
issued year-to-date in 2026, including issuance by NTT.
In addition to the €60bn of issuance from hyperscaler data centre
operators, we expect €35bn of net supply from other technology
issuers. Companies are likely to pursue acquisitions and raise
additional funding for investment, including semiconductor,

software and payment services companies. Including €8bn from
telecom issuers, this brings total TMT net supply to €103bn. This
would translate into gross supply of approximately €150bn, which,
in our view, is close to the amount the European market may be
able to absorb.
Supply discipline will be important
Companies will need to issue prudently and provide clear guidance
on future funding plans, as spreads may only tighten once the
market no longer expects substantial new supply. Nevertheless,

current spreads look attractive relative to the issuers’ high ratings,
with much of the widening driven by supply and liquidity eﬀects.
Relatively wide CDS spreads also suggest that technical factors,
rather than a material deterioration in credit risk, are weighing on
valuations. This combination of high credit quality and attractive
spreads should attract new money to the investment-grade
market.

Challenge 1: Does debt issuance reduce
credit quality?
First, we expect the hyperscaler data centre operators Amazon,
Microsoft, Alphabet, Meta and Oracle to grow their combined
EBITDA by US$230bn, or 29%, in FY27, to around US$1tr. Moreover,
with the exception of Oracle, these companies are expected to
operate with limited net debt, with Microsoft holding more cash
than debt. The main caveat is their signiﬁcant lease commitments,
many of which are not yet reﬂected in balance-sheet lease

rating agencies will attach greater weight to these liabilities,
particularly if returns on investment start to decline. In our view, this
is not a material concern for 2027 and may never become one,
given the sector’s strong growth and ability to fund a signiﬁcant
part of investment from operating cash ﬂow. Companies also retain
ﬂexibility to scale back future investment that has not yet been
committed if returns start to disappoint.
Challenge 2: Do credit benchmarks

accommodate more supply?
Do European credit markets have the capacity to absorb additional
supply from technology companies? We think the current share of
technology issuers remains modest. Alphabet accounts for only
1.16% of the iBoxx € Non-Financials index, while Amazon and IBM
account for 0.68% and 0.91%, respectively.
In the US, Oracle’s share of more than 2% in the iBoxx $ Domestic
Non-Financials index illustrates that individual technology issuers

can reach materially higher benchmark weights, while several high-

Document 398: Q3 Growth in advanced economies_ solid despite shocks (2026-10-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Q3 Growth in advanced economies_ solid despite shocks (2026-10-23).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 23 October 2026

EcoFlash

Q3 Growth in advanced economies: solid despite shocks

Growth looks to have held up in Q3 despite successive shocks (see our analysis), as it did in 2025 and H1 2026. A longer 
Middle East conflict has fed into more persistent inflation; drought has worsened in many countries, and interest rates 
have risen further – at the short end, with both the ECB and the Fed raising policy rates in September, and even more at 
the long end. However, our nowcasts show that growth is bending without breaking in most advanced economies. GDP 
figures (due on 29 October in the US and 30 October for the euro area and its member states) are expected to confirm 
that. In the Euro area, momentum looks stable (excluding Ireland, whose GDP is volatile). In Germany, growth has been 
uninterrupted since Q4 2025, while France is likely to have returned to growth after stagnating in H1. In the US, growth 
is likely to have gathered pace, according to the GDP Now, still driven by AI, but increasingly by other sectors as well.

Euro area: resilience beneath the surface. Our nowcast points to growth slowing to 0.3% in q/q, though the pace is stable 
excluding Ireland (Irish GDP is volatile and had markedly boosted the Q2 figure). Excluding Ireland, growth should hold at its H2 
average, a sign of the bloc’s underlying resilience. Also, momentum has broadened to a growing number of sectors ( see our 
analysis). While industrial production stalled over the summer, services remained buoyant. The services PMI rebounded sharply 
in Q3 and sits firmly in expansion territory (53.0 in September). Improving job-related survey data (PMI and European Commission 
surveys) and a stable unemployment rate (6.4% in August) hint at more hiring in Q3 – a support for household consumption that 
partly offsets purchasing-power losses from persistent inflation. Corporate investment is underpinned by buoyant new investment 
lending (8.7% y/y In August, 12-month cumulative). 
France: growing, nevertheless. The succession of shocks does not appear to have prevented France from returning to 
growth in Q3, at 0.2% q/q, according to our nowcast. One-off factors accounted for the stall in activity in H1. In Q3, the drought 
worsened and industrial production remained subdued. However, market services production rebounded in July and August, 
notably in business services, restaurants, digital and real estate. The latter points to residential investment recovering after a weak 
H1. Corporate investment likely followed, as reflected in buoyant credit flows and AI-related investment. Household consumption 
is likely to have improved slightly on higher electric vehicle sales.  
Euro area  France

Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas  Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas

Germany: growth still solid. The H1 rebound in activity is moderating but continues. Our nowcast points to a fourth straight 
quarter of growth in Q3, at 0.2% q/q (after 0.3% in Q4 2025, 0.4% in Q1 2026, and 0.3% in Q2). Manufacturing activity eased 
slightly, according to the September PMI survey (53.9, down 0.4 m/m). Government spending will remain growth-supportive, with 
defence and infrastructure outlays gaining traction. Infrastructure spending continues to underpin activity in public works, which 
stands at its highest since November 2021 (2.5% 3m/3m in August). Exports (4.4% y/y between January and August) remain the 
main growth engine, helped by a pivot towards Europe (7.2%). 
United States: an expansion undeterred by inflation. Growth is likely to have gathered pace in Q3. The Atlanta Fed’s GDP 
Now points to a 3.5% annualized growth rate (AR), close to our own 3.7% forecast (0.9% non -AR) and up from 2.2% in 
Q2. Domestic demand remains the engine. Consumption is weathering the pickup in inflation, while the AI-driven capex cycle 
remains robust – as reflected in retail sales and nondefence capital-goods orders (excl. aircraft). As a result, growth in final sales

### Page 2

to private domestic purchasers 1 should print above 4% AR again. By contrast, external trade should remain a drag on growth, 
reflecting AI-related import needs. Survey data point to mome ntum that is both solid and broad -based. The ISM indices have 
remained firmly in growth territory (54.9 quarterly average for manufacturing and services alike). At the same time, they highlight 
persistent price and supply-chain pressures.

Germany   United States

Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas   Source: Refinitiv, Atlanta Fed, BNP Paribas

Document 415: Taiwan Earnings Call Weekly Recap - Is Earnings Still Enough to Drive Stock Price_ _ GSTW

Source PDF: all report pdf/Taiwan Earnings Call Weekly Recap - Is Earnings Still Enough to Drive Stock Price_ _ GSTW.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-30

### Page 1

Taiwan Earnings Call Weekly Recap - Is Earnings
Still Enough to Drive Stock Price? | GSTW
30 October 2026 | 4:17 PM Taiwan Standard Time
Robert Wang
Goldman Sachs (Asia) L.L.C. Taipei Branch
FICC & Equities
Short week but busy for Taiwan in terms of earnings. Index is only down 1.8% WTD,
though multiple large caps saw over 10% drawdown in this week alone. The elevated
level in Taiwan vs other regional markets still attracted much short interest without

much buying interest except from retail. Overall sentiment is not bearish, though
investors have turned much more defensive and hiding in sectors such as Financials,
Telecom, and TSMC.
For earnings, winners' momentum was short lived; instead of chasing into the beat,
many chose to further profit take on the strength (Unimicron, EMC/TUC, Landmark
on open right after their earnings). Companies that missed the results were definitely
punished (i.e. Chroma, Delta, UMC). Echoing back to my previous note, range bound

trade seems to be the safest way to stay afloat.
See below for my single stock takeaway:
Mediatek (2454 TT)
Financial Highlights:
3Q26 revenue reached NT$168.06bn (+10% QoQ and +18% YoY) with EPS at
NT$17.5 (+15% QoQ and +11% YoY) vs cons. NT$15.95. GM came in at 46.3%
(+11ppt QoQ and 18ppt YoY) vs cons. 46.2%.
3Q26 outperformance was mainly from stronger-than-expected customer
demand and improved supply chain yield. First ASIC already started shipping since

Sep'26.
Operational Outlook:
Revenue outlook for 4Q26 is NT$210 - NT$222bn, translating to a 29% sequential
growth. 2027 revenue growth will likely grow by high single digits yoy. GM will be
around 45% with +/-1.5%, and OPM is expected to be at 26% +/-2%.
ASIC chip sales for 2026 will be around US$2.5bn, higher than previous guidance,
and it will reach US$16bn in 2027, around 20% of ASIC TAM and at the higher end
of the company guidance. Next gen chip's tape out is on track and targeted to

enter mass production in 1Q28.
Delta Electronics (2308 TT)

Financial Highlights:
3Q26 revenue reached NT$197.1bn (+8% QoQ / +31% YoY), while EPS registered
at NT$9.05. GM declined to 34.1% (down 1.5ppt QoQ), impacted by a NT$3.9bn
inventory write-down (~1.7% of revenue) mostly in Power Electronics.
Operating profit rose +7% QoQ (+31% YoY) backed by operating leverage, keeping
OPM resilient at 16.6% flat QoQ. Net profit was NT$23.5bn (-7% QoQ / +26% YoY)
due to lower non-operating investment gains.
Operational Outlook:

Management expects 4Q26 revenue to grow sequentially with gross margins
unlikely to deteriorate, aided by inventory normalization and potential price
adjustments to offset ~2% YTD material cost inflation.
AI-related exposure approaches 50% of total revenue (liquid cooling at ~13%), and
management remains confident in AI infrastructure demand through 2030 despite
near-term bottlenecks.
Key Q&A:
Inventory Write-down & Normalization: The NT$3.9bn write-down stemmed from

component mismatches and slower customer ramp-ups of newer platforms (e.g.,
18.5kW vs. 5.5kW platforms); management expects affected inventory to be
progressively consumed by 1Q27.
HVDC Adoption & Vicor Litigation: While 400V products saw partial shipments in
3Q26, 800V adoption is slower and primarily NVIDIA-driven. On the Vicor VPD
patent dispute, management expects no near-term impact on customer
relationships or shipments, with an initial ITC determination unlikely until 2027.

ASE Technology Holding (3711 TT)
Financial Highlights:
3Q26 consolidated revenue reached NT$244.16bn, with a GM of 22.1% and EPS of
NT$5.84, all outperforming market expectations.
ATM (Advanced Semiconductor Engineering, Test and Materials) GM hit 30.6%,
breaking past previous structural highs; the company announced 30% as its
baseline gross margin for the future.
Operational Outlook:
4Q26 consolidated revenue is projected to grow 6%–8% QoQ, with GM between

23.0%–24.0% and OPM between 14.5%–15.5%.
CPO is set to enter mass production in 4Q26, with related revenue expected to
contribute over US$500mn in 2027; the full-year LEAP revenue target has been
revised upward to over US$4bn.
Key Q&A:
Capex Upward Revision: Due to strong demand for LEAP and mainstream
advanced packaging, full-year 2026 capex was raised from US$10.5bn to
US$11.5bn (60% allocated to LEAP, 40% to mainstream packaging), with 2027
capex expected to be even higher.

Capacity Bottlenecks & Expansion: Factory floor space is currently the primary
constraint on near-term growth. The company is actively searching for and
acquiring existing facilities to shorten setup timelines, prioritizing resource
allocation toward packaging and wafer sort.
Elite Material (2383 TT)

GM outperformed expectations, hitting 37.7% for the quarter.
Operational Outlook:
4Q26 revenue growth is conservatively estimated to be at least 15%–20% QoQ,
with gross margins expanding further to 38.0%–39.5%.
Proactive capacity expansion plans (targeting 15.25 million sheets/month by end-
2030 vs. 5.85 million sheets/month currently) are still insufficient to support all

signed Long-Term Agreements (LTAs) for the next 4 years, pointing to a severe
supply-demand gap and zero oversupply risk.
Key Q&A:
CCL Pricing & Substrate Expansion: EMC is executing its second round of high-end
CCL (M7+) price hikes this year (5%–20% increases covering M7/M8 and M8+/M9
grades). Furthermore, it is rapidly expanding high-end ABF substrate core-layer
CCL capacity from 300k sheets/month to at least 1.2 million sheets/month by end-
2027, targeting commercial shipments starting in 4Q27.

Competition & Market Share: While AI customers are qualifying second or third
sources to secure supply chains, EMC maintains its long-term target of holding a
50% AI CCL market share, supported by best-in-class product quality and deep
CSP/PCB collaboration.
Yageo Corporation (2327 TT)
Financial Highlights:
3Q26 revenue reached NT$49.53bn (+11.4% QoQ, +49.7% YoY), with a GM of 41%
and EPS of NT$5.83.
Free cash flow surged to NT$9.236 billion, while the net financial debt-to-EBITDA

ratio dropped from 59% in 4Q25 to 24% in 3Q26, reflecting a continuously
improving financial structure and robust cash generation.
Operational Outlook:
4Q26 revenue, GM, and OPM are all expected to edge up sequentially, defying
typical seasonal flat-to-down trends for the fourth quarter.
AI demand remains solid, with its contribution to total revenue expanding from
15% in 1Q26 and 16% in 2Q26 to approximately 20% in 3Q26.
Key Q&A:
Capacity & Expansion Plans: Capacity utilization rates stood at 95% in 3Q26 and

are expected to exceed 95% in 4Q26, surpassing the healthy range of 85%–90%.
Total capacity for tantalum capacitors and MLCCs is planned to expand by 10%–
15% in 2027, with portions coming online starting in the second quarter.
Order Momentum & B/B Ratio: The overall book-to-bill (B/B) ratio in 3Q26
remained close to 2.0, with AI-related tantalum capacitors and MLCCs seeing
particularly robust orders, while LTAs with core customers continue to underpin

future shipments.
Nan Ya Plastics (1303 TT)
Financial Highlights:

3Q26 revenue rose to NT$95.6bn (+14.3% QoQ), with electronic materials
expanding to 58.1% of total sales. Operating profit surged 61.2% QoQ to
NT$18.31bn, of which electronic materials contributed 93.8%.
EPS hit a record NT$4.90, bringing 9M26 EPS to a record NT$10.07, supported by
electronic material profitability and investment income from Nanya Technology.
Operational Outlook:
CCL has surpassed NYPCB as the segment's primary revenue driver (31% of

electronic materials) with segment OPM scaling to 30.9% (up from 19.9% in 2Q26).
Management guided for fiberglass fabrics and CCL price hikes to continue at a 5–
10% MoM increase pace through early 2027, alongside a notable product mix shift
toward M7/M8 in 1Q27.
Key Q&A:
CCL Pricing and M-Series Mix: CCL and fiberglass-cloth pricing remains firm into
early 2027 with monthly 5–10% increases. M-series products reached ~30% of
CCL revenue in September, with management expecting further mix improvement

toward M7/M8 as CSP projects lock in.
Capacity Expansion and M10 Development: Monthly CCL capacity is projected to
expand from 6.18m to 6.48m sheets by year-end. For M9/M10, Nan Ya is
independently formulating hydrocarbon resins and fiberglass incorporating low-
dielectric and quartz materials with solid sampling progress.
TUC (6274 TT)
Financial Highlights:
3Q26 financial results beat market consensus, reporting revenue of NT17.95bn,
GM of 36.9%, operating margin of 31.0%, and EPS of NT$12.88, outperforming

consensus estimates by 29% to 35%.
The earnings beat was driven by continuous growth in high-end product revenue
and a return to profitability for the Thailand plant, which successfully offset earlier
first-half losses.
Operational Outlook:
For 4Q26, high-end product pricing is initiating upward adjustments. With the
Thailand plant adding another 100k sheets of capacity and all series of CCL seeing
price hike opportunities (especially high-end M7/M8 catch-up price increases

alongside tightening mid-to-low-end fiberglass cloth supply), product mix and
profitability are set to improve further.
ASIC AI servers are emerging as a core growth engine, alongside long-term
opportunities from satellite and space data centers. TUC is adding several large
ASIC customers (some already in sampling/board qualification stages) and
expanding its customer base in 400G, 800G, and 1.6T switches.
Key Q&A / Detailed Highlights:
ASIC & Switch Expansion: AI servers will serve as the primary growth driver, with

multiple major ASIC clients entering the board-maker certification stage;
meanwhile, existing 400G switch customers are rapidly accelerating 800G and 1.6T
switch certifications, with some expecting 1.6T demand to outpace 800G by 2027.
Satellite and PTFE Solutions: Space satellite PCBs utilizing M8+ materials are slated
to begin contribution in 1H27, with long-term customer demand forecasts already
extended through 2032. For PTFE, TUC boasts ~5–6 years of R&D foundation

rather than being a newcomer, though external partnerships may be pursued to
bypass special high-temperature press machine capacity limitations

Win Semiconductors (3105 TT)
Financial Highlights:
3Q26 revenue reached NT$5.86bn (+11.5% QoQ / +31% YoY) with capacity
utilization improving to 75%. GM expanded significantly by 9.2ppts QoQ to an 18-
quarter high of 37.4% (standalone GM reached 40.7%), while OPM rose to 24.2%
and EPS reached NT$4.55.
Margin expansion was primarily driven by a favorable product mix shift toward
higher-margin Infrastructure and Optical products rather than utilization gains

alone.
Operational Outlook:
Management guided 4Q26 revenue for low-single-digit QoQ growth—beating
normal smartphone seasonality—with gross margins sustaining in the high-30%
range.
Moving into FY27, photodetector (PD) production is expected to ramp materially,
while CW Laser and EML are anticipated to contribute more meaningfully starting
in FY28[cite: 388].
Key Q&A:
Infrastructure and Satellite Growth: Infrastructure became WIN's largest revenue

contributor at 35–40% in 3Q26, driven by LEO satellites, aerospace, and long-
distance communications; demand is expected to stay robust into FY27.
Optical and PD Production Timeline: Optical revenue rose QoQ on initial PD mass
production; PD will see significantly higher full-year shipment volumes in FY27,
while CW Laser and EML will generate limited initial revenue before a FY28 volume
ramp.

• The global landscape remains complex, shaped by AI, 
energy prices and tariffs. 
• Taiwan’s AI cycle remains strong, with robust AI 
exports and investment and an intact semiconductor 
competitive moat. 
• AI is generating broader domestic spillovers, with 
wealth effects and wage growth supporting 
consumption. 
• Taiwan is cushioning the energy shock by diversifying 
energy imports and absorbing much of the increase in 
upstream costs. 
• AI-driven growth is likely to moderate from 4Q26 into 
2027, as hyperscaler  cash-flow pressures rise and 
higher global rates lift investment costs. 
• Energy remains a n inflation risk, with domestic 
electricity prices facing upward pressure after the 
November local elections. 
• GDP growth is expected to moderate to high -single 
digits from 4Q26, while we maintain our 11.6%/5.6% 
GDP forecasts for 2026/27. 
• Inflation is likely to remain above 2% in 4Q26, with our 
2.0%/2.1% CPI forecasts for 2026/27 unchanged. 
• We maintain our call for CBC to hike 12.5bp to 2.125% 
in December, amid tighter liquidity conditions. 
• Housing should recover gradually despite credit 
easing, as excess new -home inventory remains a n 
overhang.

Taiwan: AI, energy and 4Q outlook October 13, 2026

Global backdrop

The global landscape remains complex, 
shaped by the interplay of AI, energy 
prices and tariffs, with significant 
implications for growth, inflation and 
financial conditions.

1) AI

The debate over the durability of the AI 
investment boom continues.  Tailwinds 
include technological advances, broader 
adoption, and s trategic and geopolitical 
considerations. Headwinds range from 
uncertainty over investment returns  and 
hyperscaler financing pressure to 
physical bottlenecks , safety concerns, 
and regulatory uncertainty.

Hyperscaler financing pressure has 
attracted increasing attention . In the 
early stages of the AI investment boom, 
hyperscaler capex was largely financed 
by internal cash flow. This is now 
changing, as free cash flow is expected 
to turn negative from 4Q, requiring major 
players to rely on debt issuance and 
other external financing increasingly.

At the same time, higher global interest 
rates are raising the cost of AI 
investment. The Fed resumed rate hikes 
at its September meeting, while global 
bond yields remain under upward 
pressure amid persistent inflation and 
elevated public and private borro wing 
needs. Our baseline assumes US rates 
will rise by 50bps this year and a further 
25bps in 2027 to 4.50%, with global 
long-term rates also remaining under 
pressure. This could increasingly weigh 
on the economics and pace of AI 
investment.

US-China AI competition is also drawing 
increasing attention.  Across the five 
layers of the AI ecosystem, China is 
making rapid progress in energy, 
infrastructure, AI models and 
applications, supported by its large scale, 
cost-effective domestic manufacturing 
supply chains and huge consumer 
market, which provides a wide range of 
real-world scenarios for AI adoption. The 
emergence of a more integrated and 
cost-competitive Chinese AI ecosystem 
could increasingly challe nge the US 
ecosystem, which currently operates at a 
relatively higher cost.

China, however, remains relatively weak 
in semiconductors.  Around half of 
China's domestic AI chip market still 
relies on foreign suppliers, including 
lower-end NVIDIA chips. This 
semiconductor bottleneck remains a key 
constraint on China's AI development. In 
our view, Taiwanese semiconductor 
companies' competiti ve moat therefore 
remains largely intact in the near term.

or semiconductor demand.  Hyperscaler

through 2Q, while Bloomberg forecasts 
growth to moderate from 95% this year 
to 39% in 2027. Similarly, global 
semiconductor sales remained strong 
through 3Q, while WSTS expects growth 
to moderate from 90% this year to 27 % 
in 2027.

2) Energy

The US -Iran conflict remains at a 
standoff, keeping the energy -price 
outlook highly uncertain.  New 
developments are likely after the US 
midterm elections in November, with 
both further escalation and de -
escalation possible. Crude oil prices have 
remained elevated at around 
USD100/bbl amid a persistent 
geopolitical risk premium . In contrast, 
refined product prices have risen more 
sharply as refining disruptions and 
tighter product logistics have amplified 
the supply shock. Natural gas prices 
have also increased sharply, reflecting 
disruptions to LNG supply and limited 
alternative shipping routes.

Taiwan has been actively diversifying 
its energy import sources to cushion the 
impact. LNG imports from Qatar have 
fallen to zero since April, while imports 
from the US and other suppliers have 
surged to offset the shortfall, helping 
maintain stable domestic electricity 
supply.

3) Tariffs

US tariff actions are likely to accelerate 
after the US midterm elections in 
November. Two major tariff measures 
remain in the pipeline. The Section 301 
forced-labor tariff was implemented in 
July, while final tariff action under the 
Section 301 overcapacity investigation, 
which covers 16 economies, remains 
pending. Meanwhile, Phase 1 of the 
Section 232 semiconductor tariffs was 
implemented in January, while Phase 2, 
covering a broader range of 
semiconductor-related products, 
remains pending.

Taiwan has secured a relatively 
favourable framework through its trade 
and investment agreements with the 
US. Taiwan signed an Agreement on 
Reciprocal Trade (ART) and an 
Investment MOU with the US earlier this 
year. The ART establishes a 15% US tariff 
rate on Taiwanese goods, while the MOU 
provides Taiwan with preferential 
treatment under Section 232, including 
for semiconductors. The ART has yet to 
enter into force, and it remains to be 
seen how the final Section 301 tariff 
burden will interact with the 15% ART 
framework. Meanwhile, major Taiwanese 
companies such as TSMC are expanding 
their US investments under the 
Investment MOU, which could support 
relatively favourable treatment for 
Taiwan when broader Section 232 
semiconductor tariffs are introduced.

Taiwan 4Q outlook

Taiwan has navigated the complex 
global environment relatively well so far 
this year, supported by robust AI exports 
and investment, diversified energy 
imports, and efforts to absorb higher 
upstream energy costs. Some 
normalisation is likely in 4Q26 –2027, as 
the AI -driven growth boost moderates 
and domestic energy prices face greater 
pressure to adjust.

1) Growth

GDP growth remains broadly on track 
with our expectations. High -frequency 
indicators suggest that GDP growth 
should remain around 12% yoy in 3Q, 
broadly in line with 12.9% in 2Q but below 
the 15.4% peak in 1Q. Growth is expected 
to moderate to a high -single-digit pace 
from 4Q onwards. We maintain our full -
year GDP growth forecasts at 11.6% for 
2026 and 5.6% for 2027.

The AI cycle remains the key driver of 
Taiwan's growth.  Information and 
communication products and electronic 
components accounted for nearly 80% 
of total exports in the first nine months 
of this year, while manufacturing 
accounts for nearly 50% of gross fixed

capital formation. From 4Q onwards, 
export growth is expected to moderate 
from exceptionally strong levels to a 
more normal but still robust pace, 
broadly following the trajectory of 
global hyperscaler capex and 
semiconductor sales. Investment is likely 
to follow a similar path.

For now, Taiwanese technology 
companies continue to report robust 
revenue growth, led by semiconductors, 
ODMs/EMS, and PCs and servers. 
Capacity utilisation at leading Taiwanese 
foundries has also continued to rise, 
suggesting that investment demand 
remains strong.

The AI boom is also beginning to 
generate broader spillovers to the 
domestic economy. The AI-driven equity 
rally has generated significant wealth 
effects, with the TAIEX up 70% year-to-
date and Taiwan becoming the world's 
fifth-largest stock market. Taiwanese 
households have benefited from the 
appreciation of financial assets, with 
equities and mutual funds accounting for 
nearly 30% of total household financial 
assets. The equity rally has also 
generated positive income effects. Wage 
growth has picked up, led by the services 
sector, particularly financial services, as 
strong earnings at financial 
institutions—especially securities 
firms—have prompted higher 
compensation to retain talent. These 
wealth and income gains should support 
household consumption, creating a 
positive feedback loop that broadens the 
benefits of the AI boom across the wider 
economy.

                                                                                                                                            7

2) Inflation

CPI inflation is also tracking broadly in 
line with our forecast. Headline CPI 
remained elevated at 2.4% yoy in 3Q, up 
from 1.2% in 1Q and 2.2% in 2Q. We 
maintain our forecast for headline CPI 
inflation to remain close to 2.5% in 4Q, 
and retain our full-year forecasts at 2.0% 
for 2026 and 2.1% for 2027.

Energy inflation remains the main 
upside risk.  The government has 
absorbed much of the increase in energy 
costs through state -owned CPC and 
Taipower, limiting the pass -through to 
domestic consumers. With losses at both 
companies widening, the government 
will need to decide whether to provide 
additional subsidies to cover the losses 
or allow further increases in domestic 
energy prices. The September re view 
postponed the adjustment to the 
December meeting, when the overall 
situation will be reassessed after the 
November local elections. Taipower's  
latest review indicated that electricity 
rates would need to rise by 12.8%. A hike 
in electricity prices could lift inflation 
expectations and generate second -
round effects, particularly as the labour 
market tightens and wage pressures 
increase.

3) Monetary policy

The central bank kept the policy 
discount rate unchanged at 2.00% at its 
September meeting, in line with our 
expectations. We continue to expect a 
modest 12.5bp rate hike at the 
December meeting, bringing the policy 
rate to 2.125%.  By then, CPI inflation is 
likely to have remained above 2% for 
around six consecutive months. The 
possibility of an increase in electricity 
prices following the November local 
elections could further lift inflation 
expectations, strengthening the case for 
a December rate hike.

Financial conditions have already 
tightened despite the absence of a 
policy rate hike.  M2 growth remained 
around 7% yoy in 3Q, well below double-
digit nominal GDP growth. At the same 
time, bank loan growth has accelerated 
to more than 10% yoy, driven by strong

                                                                                                                                            8

corporate credit demand, particularly AI-
related fundraising by technology 
companies. Tighter financial conditions 
are also evident in rising market interest 
rates, including commercial paper rates 
and government bond yields.

The central bank has continued to ease 
housing credit controls gradually. At its 
September meeting, it raised the LTV 
cap on second -home mortgage loans to 
70%, the second easing of housing credit 
controls this year. There is scope for 
further easing, including removing the 
LTV cap on second-home loans or raising 
the LTV caps for third - and fourth-home 
loans and high -value properties, which 
currently stand at 30%. However, the 
central bank is likely to proceed 
cautiously, given concerns that a faster 
relaxation could reignite  speculative 
demand and put renewed upward 
pressure on housing prices.

4) Housing

A gradual and modest recovery in the 
housing market is expected from 4Q26 
into 2027.  The existing -home market 
has already shown early signs of 
recovery, with Sinyi  housing prices 
returning to slightly positive yoy growth 
since 2Q. Total building transactions also 
returned to positive yoy growth in 3Q. 
The easing of credit controls should help 
reinforce this modest recovery trend.

The new -home market remains under 
pressure. The number of new units under 
construction has started to decline since 
2023, while new completions are  only 
beginning to ease this year . Demand 
remains weak, as potential buyers 
continue to wait for lower prices. Unsold 
new homes stood at 113K units at end -
2025, compared with a historical normal 
level of around 80K, implying excess 
inventory of roughly 30K units. At the 
current pace of new -home transactions, 
it would take roughly one year to 
normalize the existing excess inventory, 
and longer when factoring in the new 
supply pipeline. Some industry experts 
estimate that it could take as long as 3.8 
years digest current inventories fully 
(here).

At a Glance: Geopolitical events to watch in October 2026
Our monthly Look-Ahead covers a non-exhaustive list of key events, issues and themes to watch in the month ahead. The below map intends to serve as an
executive summary, providing a list of the issues at a glance. These are covered in more detail in subsequent slides. For an overview of key events between

October and December 2026, see our latest 90-day Geopolitical Risk Radar.

### Page 3

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026
Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Diplomacy appears firmly back in play. The past month saw continued fighting and renewed diplomacy.
Reporting suggests that the US is prepared to extend troop deployments in the Middle East to 2027. Iran
targeted both US forces in the region and some tankers moving through the Strait, but has been unable to
prevent a significant rebound in crude flows through Hormuz. On 22 SEP, talks were mediated by Qatar,

Pakistan and Egypt, and on 24 SEP, Iran presented the US with a seven-day roadmap to a ceasefire leading
to a phased reopening of the Strait of Hormuz and a return to nuclear negotiations, conditional on a US
lifting of its blockade of Iran, sanctions relief and broader cessation of hostilities. The Trump
Administration rejected the proposal, but negotiations continue.
To watch will be whether October is the month the war transitions into a negotiated settlement, which

may hinge on whether Iran is willing to make some concessions on sanctions relief and the nuclear
question, and whether Trump is keen to demonstrate that the war has produced a political and economic
result in the final weeks before the 3 NOV midterm elections. It is plausible that the current diplomatic
channel could produce a temporary de-escalation mechanism as long as there is pathway around the
broader nuclear issue.
Both sides will reconvene for trade talks in Beijing on 8-9 October, which the EU views as a deadline for

“tangible results”. Brussels remains focused on its rising trade deficit with China – which has reached a
record €1 billion per day – and the impact of Chinese goods on European industry. The bloc has vowed to
take measures if talks fail.
While both sides could achieve progress in talks, a lasting breakthrough appears improbable, setting the
stage for more trade tensions into 2027. New EU trade investigations, curbs on Chinese imports, and EU-
level instruments intended to address alleged Chinese overcapacity, are possible outcomes, to which

Beijing has stated it would respond.
China-EU trade talks could also impact China-US relations. Should talks with Brussels fall short of averting
increased European trade pressure, we assess that Beijing may further prioritise near-term US stability. See
Rules of Engagement: Mapping the Next Phase of China-US Ties for more.
In the spotlight for October 2026: US-Iran war and EU-China relations

### Page 4

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026
Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Latvia will elect all 100 members of the 15th Saeima. Latvia, which borders Russia and Belarus, has been
among the most vocal supporters of Ukraine. The next government will set the country’s course on
defence spending, Ukraine aid, sanctions enforcement and border security. The field is highly fragmented
with the governing parties lacking a majority in the polls. The fragmentation matters as a weakened and

multi-party scramble could mean a slower, more unstable coalition.
Brazil’s first electoral round is on 4 OCT, with a runoff on 25 OCT. This is a critical election as it will
decide whether Latin America’s biggest economy continues President Lula’s state-led social spending or
pivots to a more fiscally-orthodox agenda under Bolsonaro. Polls have gone from a Lula lead in the spring
to a statistical dead heat between Lula and Bolsonaro. A Brazilian Supreme Court crisis – which has

implicated Supreme Court Justices and politicians on both sides – is weighing on the race. Congressional
and gubernatorial races will also impact the governability of the country.
This election will also shape whether the country hedges between Washington and Beijing under Lula or
tilts toward closer US trade and security alignment under Bolsonaro with potential implications for
tariffs, BRICS+ cohesion, and Latin America’s broader positioning between the two powers.

The meetings will take place in Bangkok, bringing finance ministers, central bankers, policy-makers and
business leaders together. The agenda is expected to centre on global growth, financial stability, debt,
trade and investment, development finance and the impact of AI on jobs and productivity, with particular
attention given to mobilizing private capital. The key watchpoint will be the outlook for global growth
and inflation, debt vulnerabilities, the future of the international financial architecture and how policy-

makers respond to increasing fragmentation in trade and capital flows.
Key events, issues and themes to watch in October 2026
Listed in chronological order

### Page 5

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026
Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Leaders are expected to focus heavily on Ukraine, European security and defence, competitiveness and
the EU’s economic position in a more contested global environment. They will also take forward
negotiations on the 2028-2034 EU long-term budget. They set themselves a soft deadline in June to get
the budget broadly locked down because missing it pushes ratification into 2027 and risks a funding gap

for beneficiaries from JAN 2028. To watch will be a genuine breakthrough on the multiannual framework
or more likely what “substantial” progress may look like. Ukraine funding is the other pressure point. This
meeting will be important for understanding the direction of European defence spending and industrial
policy, the next phase of budget negotiations and the EU response to growing external economic
pressure and strategic dependencies.
The Canadian province of Alberta will hold a referendum, which includes a question about the province’s

future within Canada. This is not an independence vote itself but asking voters to choose between
remaining a Canadian province and asking the Alberta government to begin the constitutional process
required for a future binding referendum on separation. Current polling suggests “remain” is comfortably
ahead. The significance is around potential renewed uncertainty around Canada’s federal cohesion,
energy policy, fiscal transfers and Alberta's relationship with Ottawa and Washington. Even a modest

agreement to begin a process towards secession would likely keep alive equalization grievances as a
structural fault line in Canadian federalism feeding into political risk.
.
The event’s theme focuses on “Advancing Alliances in a New Era.” It will tackle how governments and
businesses respond to a world where geopolitics, economic security, supply chains and technology are
increasingly intertwined. There is likely to be a strong focus on the future of the transatlantic

relationship, including how Europe adapts to a more demanding security environment. There will also be
discussions of tariffs, supply chain resilience, critical capabilities and the weaponization of economic
interdependence. It will align with Chancellor Merz’s efforts to ensure Europe actively shapes outcomes
around strategic autonomy and reducing China exposure in manufacturing.
Key events, issues and themes to watch in October 2026
Listed in chronological order

### Page 6

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026
Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
The second summit will take place in Saudi Arabi, bringing EU and Gulf leaders together. The agenda is
expected to focus on regional security and the Middle East, energy and economic security, trade and
investment, infrastructure, technology and broader strategic cooperation. The security dimension will be
particularly salient given the current crisis in the region. Economically, the relationship is substantial: EU-

GCC trade reached €165.6bn in 2025.
To watch will be movement on the long-stalled EU-GCC free trade talks and visa-waiver requests from
Qatar and Saudi Arabia in addition to the extent of the focus on Gaza reconstruction, Iran and regional
security get relative to the economic agenda.
GCC states have consistently used these summits to press for clearer EU positioning on Middle East
conflicts in exchange for economic cooperation. The summit comes amid at a critical juncture where

Europe is recalibrating its external partnerships.
The Central Committee of the Communist Party of China will hold its Fifth Plenary Session in Beijing.
This will be one of the most important events on the country’s political calendar ahead of the 21st Party
Congress in late 2027.
The Plenum will focus on Party governance, and investors will scrutinise signals regarding leadership
changes and personnel reshuffles, institutional reforms, and political priorities for the next Congress.

While fifth plenums historically focus on economic planning, the development guidelines for the 15th
Five-Year Plan (2026–2030) were already adopted during the Fourth Plenum in OCT 2025, allowing the
upcoming session to focus on personnel and governance matters.
Key events, issues and themes to watch in October 2026
Listed in chronological order
Summit between the EU and the Gulf Cooperation Council (24 October)
Source: istockphoto
Fifth Plenum of China’s Central Committee (26-29 October)
